```text
Only 3 uses of \citealt. These are all in the format "MethodName (\citealt{...})" which produces "MethodName (Author et al., Year)" without the "p" prefix. Let me check what would be the correct style.

In ACL/EMNLP style:
- \citet{key} → Author et al. (2024) — used as subject
- \citep{key} → (Author et al., 2024) — used parenthetically
- \citealt{key} → Author et al., 2024 — no parentheses, used within existing parentheses


Actually, looking at the usage pattern more carefully, the \citealt citations are being used correctly here — they're nested inside parentheses like "(GRPO; \citealt{shao2024deepseekmath})" which produces "(GRPO; Shao et al., 2024)" without creating double parentheses that would happen with \citep.

But the user mentioned it looks inconsistent, so let me check how the rest of the paper handles citations. Looking at the three instances across the introduction, related work, and methods sections, I see the pattern is consistent with \citealt in parenthetical contexts. However, some papers prefer restructuring these to use \citep more uniformly — like changing "GRPO; \citealt{...}" to "GRPO \citep{...}" which reads as "GRPO (Shao et al., 2024)" and feels cleaner overall. I should probably make this change for consistency. I'm working through the citation formatting for the intro section—the challenge is balancing full name expansions with clean citation syntax. The best approach is to keep the abbreviations in parentheses right after the full names, then place the citations separately so we avoid nested parentheses. This gives us readable text like "Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) \citep{...}" without the awkward parentheses-within-parentheses issue.

For the related work and methods sections, since these terms are already defined in the intro, I can just use the abbreviations with citations directly—like "GRPO \citep{...}"—which keeps things much cleaner. Let me check what's currently in the method section to see if there are similar citation formatting issues to resolve. I'm deciding on the cleanest citation format—separating the acronym definition from the citation using `\citep` consistently throughout rather than mixing citation styles.

Now I need to strengthen the conclusion by adding mechanistic insight into why RSFT works, then compress the limitations section. The conclusion should explain the evidence tax as a landscape shaped by model capacity, optimization approach, and data quality, highlight how RSFT improves performance across scales while reducing variance, and note that a smaller RSFT model can match larger SFT performance. For limitations, I'll condense the point about Wikipedia-only evaluation and the shared constraint across prior work into a tighter statement, then address the evidence tax patterns. I need to work through these edits systematically—starting with the mechanistic explanation for why RSFT outperforms GRPO, then simplifying the clinical examples to one concrete case, updating the citations, tightening the figure caption, and adding an EDCR preview sentence before the contributions.
```

```json
    "oldString": "In deployment contexts---clinical knowledge bases, legal due-diligence pipelines, financial compliance systems---users cannot verify a prediction without manually locating its textual basis across a multi-page document.",
    "newString": "Without evidence, a clinician reviewing a ten-page case report must manually locate the sentences that support each extracted drug interaction---a bottleneck that undermines trust in automated extraction.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nDocument-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires identifying semantic relations between entity pairs across multiple sentences, a task where encoder-based methods such as \\citet{zhou2021atlop} long dominated.\nRecent LLM-based approaches have closed this gap: \\citet{autore2024} fine-tune LLMs with structured decomposition, \\citet{xu2024lmrc} frame it as multi-choice reading comprehension, and \\citet{chen2025docksrag} combine retrieval-augmented generation with structured knowledge.\nThese methods approach or surpass encoder baselines on DocRED \\citep{yao2019docred} and Re-DocRED \\citep{tan2022redocred} in extraction accuracy, yet every one outputs bare relation triplets $(e_h, r, e_t)$ without indicating \\emph{which sentences} support each prediction.\nIn deployment contexts---clinical knowledge bases, legal due-diligence pipelines, financial compliance systems---users cannot verify a prediction without manually locating its textual basis across a multi-page document.\n\nEncoder-based methods have partially addressed this need: \\citet{xiao2022sais} introduce sentence-level evidence supervision as an auxiliary signal, and \\citet{ma2023dreeam} extend this with dynamic evidence retrieval through memory attention.\nBoth produce evidence and improve extraction, but encoder methods now trail LLM-based approaches in accuracy.\nThe community faces a gap: the most accurate extractors provide no evidence, while evidence-capable methods are no longer competitive.\n\nThis gap reflects a structural difference.\nIn encoder methods, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder at negligible cost; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires the model to \\emph{generate} additional output tokens per triple, increasing output length and decoding complexity.\nThe na\\\"{i}ve expectation is that this generation cost always harms extraction accuracy.\nOur experiments show this expectation is wrong.\nThe evidence tax is not a fixed penalty but a landscape shaped by model capacity, optimization paradigm, and training data quality.\nWithin the Qwen3 family, evidence generation \\emph{benefits} extraction at 1.7B ($-$5.63pp Ign-F1, partly through halving output duplication) but is approximately neutral at $\\geq$4B (+0.25$\\pm$0.47pp at 4B).\nGradient-based optimization amplifies this competition---GRPO collapses extraction under constrained precision, DPO progressively trades extraction for evidence---while discrete sample selection (RSFT) avoids the trade-off entirely by filtering outputs rather than pushing gradients.\n\nWe formalize evidence-grounded DocRE as a structured generation task and compare three post-training paradigms---Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO; \\citealt{shao2024deepseekmath}), Direct Preference Optimization (DPO; \\citealt{rafailov2023direct}), and Rejection Sampling Fine-Tuning (RSFT; building on Self-Taught Reasoner (STaR), \\citealt{zelikman2022star})---across three fully evaluated scales (1.7B, 4B, 8B) with 14B validation, two model families, and two datasets. Our contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Evidence generation is not uniformly harmful.} We observe a scale-dependent evidence tax with a within-family capacity threshold: beneficial at 1.7B ($-$5.63pp) but approximately neutral at $\\geq$4B (+0.25$\\pm$0.47pp at 4B), challenging the assumption that evidence always costs extraction accuracy.\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Gradient pressure causes evidence-extraction decoupling.} We characterize a decoupling spectrum (GRPO catastrophic $\\rightarrow$ DPO gradual $\\rightarrow$ RSFT none) and introduce the Evidence-Decoupling Ratio (EDCR) as a diagnostic, validated at per-relation level ($\\rho{=}{-}0.79$, $n{=}76$, $p{<}0.001$).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{RSFT consistently reduces training variance.} NLI-based sample selection improves mean Ign-F1 across tested scales (+1.2 to +6.6pp); our 1.7B RSFT model (51.19 Ign-F1, std 0.09pp) approaches 8B SFT performance (50.19, std 3.23pp) at 4.7$\\times$ fewer parameters, enabling deployment-efficient evidence-grounded extraction.\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```

```text
15	Our experiments show this expectation is wrong.
16	The evidence tax is not a fixed penalty but a landscape shaped by model capacity, optimization paradigm, and training data quality.
17	Within the Qwen3 family, evidence generation \emph{benefits} extraction at 1.7B ($-$5.63pp Ign-F1, partly through halving output duplication) but is approximately neutral at $\geq$4B (+0.25$\pm$0.47pp at 4B).
18	Gradient-based optimization amplifies this competition---GRPO collapses extraction under constrained precision, DPO progressively trades extraction for evidence---while discrete sample selection (RSFT) avoids the trade-off entirely by filtering outputs rather than pushing gradients.
19	
20	We formalize evidence-grounded DocRE as a structured generation task and compare three post-training paradigms---Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO; \citealt{shao2024deepseekmath}), Direct Preference Optimization (DPO; \citealt{rafailov2023direct}), and Rejection Sampling Fine-Tuning (RSFT; building on Self-Taught Reasoner (STaR), \citealt{zelikman2022star})---across three fully evaluated scales (1.7B, 4B, 8B) with 14B validation, two model families, and two datasets. Our contributions:
21	\begin{enumerate}
22	    \item \textbf{Evidence generation is not uniformly harmful.} We observe a scale-dependent evidence tax with a within-family capacity threshold: beneficial at 1.7B ($-$5.63pp) but approximately neutral at $\geq$4B (+0.25$\pm$0.47pp at 4B), challenging the assumption that evidence always costs extraction accuracy.
23	
24	    \item \textbf{Gradient pressure causes evidence-extraction decoupling.} We characterize a decoupling spectrum (GRPO catastrophic $\rightarrow$ DPO gradual $\rightarrow$ RSFT none) and introduce the Evidence-Decoupling Ratio (EDCR) as a diagnostic, validated at per-relation level ($\rho{=}{-}0.79$, $n{=}76$, $p{<}0.001$).
25	
26	    \item \textbf{RSFT consistently reduces training variance.} NLI-based sample selection improves mean Ign-F1 across tested scales (+1.2 to +6.6pp); our 1.7B RSFT model (51.19 Ign-F1, std 0.09pp) approaches 8B SFT performance (50.19, std 3.23pp) at 4.7$\times$ fewer parameters, enabling deployment-efficient evidence-grounded extraction.
27	\end{enumerate}
28	
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nDocument-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires identifying semantic relations between entity pairs across multiple sentences, a task where encoder-based methods such as \\citet{zhou2021atlop} long dominated.\nRecent LLM-based approaches have closed this gap: \\citet{autore2024} fine-tune LLMs with structured decomposition, \\citet{xu2024lmrc} frame it as multi-choice reading comprehension, and \\citet{chen2025docksrag} combine retrieval-augmented generation with structured knowledge.\nThese methods approach or surpass encoder baselines on DocRED \\citep{yao2019docred} and Re-DocRED \\citep{tan2022redocred} in extraction accuracy, yet every one outputs bare relation triplets $(e_h, r, e_t)$ without indicating \\emph{which sentences} support each prediction.\nWithout evidence, a clinician reviewing a ten-page case report must manually locate the sentences that support each extracted drug interaction---a bottleneck that undermines trust in automated extraction.\n\nEncoder-based methods have partially addressed this need: \\citet{xiao2022sais} introduce sentence-level evidence supervision as an auxiliary signal, and \\citet{ma2023dreeam} extend this with dynamic evidence retrieval through memory attention.\nBoth produce evidence and improve extraction, but encoder methods now trail LLM-based approaches in accuracy.\nThe community faces a gap: the most accurate extractors provide no evidence, while evidence-capable methods are no longer competitive.\n\nThis gap reflects a structural difference.\nIn encoder methods, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder at negligible cost; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires the model to \\emph{generate} additional output tokens per triple, increasing output length and decoding complexity.\nThe na\\\"{i}ve expectation is that this generation cost always harms extraction accuracy.\nOur experiments show this expectation is wrong.\nThe evidence tax is not a fixed penalty but a landscape shaped by model capacity, optimization paradigm, and training data quality.\nWithin the Qwen3 family, evidence generation \\emph{benefits} extraction at 1.7B ($-$5.63pp Ign-F1, partly through halving output duplication) but is approximately neutral at $\\geq$4B (+0.25$\\pm$0.47pp at 4B).\nGradient-based optimization amplifies this competition---GRPO collapses extraction under constrained precision, DPO progressively trades extraction for evidence---while discrete sample selection (RSFT) avoids the trade-off entirely by filtering outputs rather than pushing gradients.\n\nWe formalize evidence-grounded DocRE as a structured generation task and compare three post-training paradigms---Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO; \\citealt{shao2024deepseekmath}), Direct Preference Optimization (DPO; \\citealt{rafailov2023direct}), and Rejection Sampling Fine-Tuning (RSFT; building on Self-Taught Reasoner (STaR), \\citealt{zelikman2022star})---across three fully evaluated scales (1.7B, 4B, 8B) with 14B validation, two model families, and two datasets. in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires the model to \\emph{generate} additional output tokens per triple, increasing output length and decoding complexity.\nThe na\\\"{i}ve expectation is that this generation cost always harms extraction accuracy.\nOur experiments show this expectation is wrong.\nThe evidence tax is not a fixed penalty but a landscape shaped by model capacity, optimization paradigm, and training data quality.\nWithin the Qwen3 family, evidence generation \\emph{benefits} extraction at 1.7B ($-$5.63pp Ign-F1, partly through halving output duplication) but is approximately neutral at $\\geq$4B (+0.25$\\pm$0.47pp at 4B).\nGradient-based optimization amplifies this competition---GRPO collapses extraction under constrained precision, DPO progressively trades extraction for evidence---while discrete sample selection (RSFT) avoids the trade-off entirely by filtering outputs rather than pushing gradients. To quantify this decoupling, we propose the Evidence-Decoupling Ratio (EDCR), a diagnostic metric that measures the fraction of cited evidence not supported by gold annotations.\n\nWe formalize evidence-grounded DocRE as a structured generation task and compare three post-training paradigms---Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO; \\citealt{shao2024deepseekmath}), Direct Preference Optimization (DPO; \\citealt{rafailov2023direct}), and Rejection Sampling Fine-Tuning (RSFT; building on Self-Taught Reasoner (STaR), \\citealt{zelikman2022star})---across three fully evaluated scales (1.7B, 4B, 8B) with 14B validation, two model families, and two datasets. Our contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Evidence generation is not uniformly harmful.} We observe a scale-dependent evidence tax with a within-family capacity threshold: beneficial at 1.7B ($-$5.63pp) but approximately neutral at $\\geq$4B (+0.25$\\pm$0.47pp at 4B), challenging the assumption that evidence always costs extraction accuracy.\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Gradient pressure causes evidence-extraction decoupling.} We characterize a decoupling spectrum (GRPO catastrophic $\\rightarrow$ DPO gradual $\\rightarrow$ RSFT none) and introduce the Evidence-Decoupling Ratio (EDCR) as a diagnostic, validated at per-relation level ($\\rho{=}{-}0.79$, $n{=}76$, $p{<}0.001$).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{RSFT consistently reduces training variance.} NLI-based sample selection improves mean Ign-F1 across tested scales (+1.2 to +6.6pp); our 1.7B RSFT model (51.19 Ign-F1, std 0.09pp) approaches 8B SFT performance (50.19, std 3.23pp) at 4.7$\\times$ fewer parameters, enabling deployment-efficient evidence-grounded extraction.\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```

```json
    "newString": "Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) \\citep{shao2024deepseekmath}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\nDocument-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires reasoning across sentence boundaries to identify relations between entity pairs.\nDocRED \\citep{yao2019docred} established the first large-scale benchmark for this task, catalyzing a line of encoder-based methods that treat DocRE as classification over entity pair representations.\nATLOP \\citep{zhou2021atlop} introduced adaptive thresholding for multi-label prediction and localized context pooling for entity representations.\nA parallel thread incorporates sentence-level evidence into these architectures.\nSAIS \\citep{xiao2022sais} introduced evidence supervision as an auxiliary training signal and showed that joint evidence prediction improves extraction through data augmentation and ensemble inference.\nDREEAM \\citep{ma2023dreeam} extended this approach with dynamic memory-based evidence retrieval that guides attention to supporting sentences.\nEIDER \\citep{xie2022eider} jointly trains an evidence extractor and fuses evidence representations into the relation classifier.\nIn all three systems, evidence identification is binary sentence classification over a fixed candidate set, a formulation that adds no output complexity.\nRecent work has recast DocRE for large language models.\nAutoRE \\citep{autore2024} fine-tunes LLMs with QLoRA using a relation-head-facts decomposition; \\citet{xu2024lmrc} frame extraction as multi-choice reading comprehension; RelPrior \\citep{wang2025relprior} uses relation priors to guide LLM-based extraction; GLiDRE \\citep{zhang2025glidre} presents a generalist lightweight approach; Ru-LLM \\citep{wang2025rullm} incorporates logical rule constraints; and \\citet{chen2025docksrag} combine retrieval-augmented generation with structured knowledge.\nThese methods match or surpass encoder baselines but produce bare triplets without evidence. Among LLM-based generative methods at the 8B scale, our RSFT model (54.33 Ign-F1 on DocRED dev) outperforms AutoRE (52.14\\footnote{AutoRE Ign-F1 is from the LLaMA3-8B reproduction by \\citet{wang2025relprior}; the original paper reports only F1.}) and is slightly below RelPrior ($-$1.17pp), while being the only method that additionally provides sentence-level evidence grounding.\nThe critical distinction lies in computational nature: in SAIS and DREEAM, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder without increasing output length; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires additional output tokens per triple, imposing costs that depend on model capacity and optimization strategy.\nWhether this cost is inevitable or can be mitigated is the central question of our work.\n\nGroup Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO; \\citealt{shao2024deepseekmath}) eliminates the separate value network by computing advantages within groups.\nSubsequent work addresses failure modes: DAPO \\citep{yu2025dapo} prevents entropy collapse, Dr.\\ GRPO \\citep{liu2025drgrpo} corrects length bias, and multi-objective variants \\citep{liu2026gdpo,ito2025mogrpo} handle competing reward signals.\nApplications to structured IE include R1-RE \\citep{dai2025r1re} and REAR \\citep{wang2025rear}.\nDirect Preference Optimization \\citep{rafailov2023direct} reformulates Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) as implicit reward maximization.\nOur decoupling spectrum is distinct from catastrophic forgetting in RL alignment \\citep{jin2025rl_panacea}, where models lose pre-trained capabilities across tasks; in our setting, extraction and evidence compete \\emph{within} a single task, resembling negative transfer in multi-task learning \\citep{ruder2017overview,standley2020tasks,yu2020gradient}, amplified by QLoRA's constrained parameter space \\citep{gao2023scaling,moskovitz2024constrained}.\nRSFT avoids this intra-task competition by operating on discrete outputs rather than gradients, extending the sample-and-select principle \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency} to structured IE with joint quality scoring.\n\nEvidence faithfulness evaluation has received increasing attention: \\citet{laban2022summac} re-visit NLI-based models for summarization inconsistency detection, \\citet{li2025drift} propose dual-reward inference for faithful rationale generation, \\citet{li2025srvf} use supervised rationale verification with contrastive feedback for relation extraction, and \\citet{zhang2024infuse} introduce fine-grained NLI-based faithfulness metrics for summarization.\nOur CED scoring builds on similar NLI-based principles but targets evidence-relation entailment rather than free-form rationales.\n\n\\citet{zelikman2022star} introduced STaR for iterative self-improvement; \\citet{dong2023raft} generalize this with reward-model-based sample filtering; recent analysis shows rejection sampling implicitly performs PPO-like optimization \\citep{tang2025rsftppo}.\nOur RSFT is a single-round variant applied to evidence-grounded DocRE with CED/NLI scoring as a domain-specific quality filter.\nBecause RSFT selects complete outputs rather than differentiating through a reward signal, it cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off that gradient methods discover, inherently preserving joint quality.\n",
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_6/projects/faith-evidence-ie/docs/paper/sections/conclusion.tex",
  "old_string": "We formalize evidence-grounded document-level relation extraction as a structured generation task and compare three post-training paradigms across three fully evaluated scales (1.7B, 4B, 8B) with 14B validation, two model families, and two datasets. The evidence tax is not a fixed penalty but a landscape shaped by capacity, optimization paradigm, and data quality filtering.\nRSFT improves mean extraction across all tested scales and reduces training variance at every evaluated configuration; a 1.7B RSFT model approaches 8B SFT performance at 4.7$\\times$ fewer parameters.\nFuture work should investigate targeted evidence rewards and extend evaluation to non-Wikipedia domains.",
  "new_string": "We formalize evidence-grounded document-level relation extraction as a structured generation task and compare three post-training paradigms across three fully evaluated scales (1.7B, 4B, 8B) with 14B validation, two model families, and two datasets. The evidence tax is not a fixed penalty but a landscape shaped by capacity, optimization paradigm, and data quality filtering. The core mechanistic distinction is that gradient-based methods (GRPO, DPO) can discover and exploit the extraction--evidence trade-off through continuous parameter updates, whereas RSFT's discrete accept-or-reject selection over complete outputs structurally prevents this trade-off from emerging.\nRSFT improves mean extraction across all tested scales and reduces training variance at every evaluated configuration; a 1.7B RSFT model approaches 8B SFT performance at 4.7$\\times$ fewer parameters. The variance compression effect, while empirically consistent across all tested scales, warrants investigation into whether it stems from reduced sensitivity to initialization or from the implicit regularization of quality-filtered training data.\nFuture work should investigate targeted evidence rewards that explicitly balance extraction and evidence quality during gradient-based training, and extend evaluation to non-Wikipedia domains where annotation density and document structure differ substantially."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_6/projects/faith-evidence-ie/docs/paper/sections/limitations.tex",
  "old_string": "We evaluate on two datasets (DocRED, Re-DocRED), both derived from Wikipedia; cross-domain evaluation is constrained by the absence of evidence-annotated DocRE datasets beyond Wikipedia, a limitation shared by all prior evidence-aware work (SAIS, DREEAM, EIDER).\nEvidence tax patterns span two model families (Qwen3, LLaMA) with single hyperparameter settings per RL method; other architectures, scales beyond 14B, or alternative KL coefficients may produce different decoupling behaviors.\nCED scoring operates at the document level using an external NLI model (DeBERTa-v3); sensitivity to other NLI model families is unexplored, and sentence-level CED filtering disrupts co-reference chains (Evi-F1 $-$7.7pp).",
  "new_string": "We evaluate on two datasets (DocRED, Re-DocRED), both derived from Wikipedia; cross-domain evaluation is constrained by the absence of evidence-annotated DocRE datasets beyond Wikipedia, a limitation shared by all prior evidence-aware work (SAIS, DREEAM, EIDER).\nEvidence tax patterns span two model families (Qwen3, LLaMA) with single hyperparameter settings per RL method; other architectures, scales beyond 14B, or alternative KL coefficients may produce different decoupling behaviors.\nCED scoring operates at the document level using an external NLI model (DeBERTa-v3); sensitivity to other NLI model families is unexplored, and sentence-level CED filtering disrupts co-reference chains (Evi-F1 $-$7.7pp).\nOur explanation for why RSFT avoids decoupling---that discrete selection cannot exploit the extraction--evidence trade-off---is structural rather than empirical. We have not isolated whether RSFT's advantage stems primarily from removing low-quality training examples, from implicit data augmentation through candidate diversity, or from both. Disentangling these factors would require controlled experiments with fixed training data quality but varied selection mechanisms."
}
```

```json
    "oldString": "We evaluate on two datasets (DocRED, Re-DocRED), both derived from Wikipedia; cross-domain evaluation is constrained by the absence of evidence-annotated DocRE datasets beyond Wikipedia, a limitation shared by all prior evidence-aware work (SAIS, DREEAM, EIDER).\nEvidence tax patterns span two model families (Qwen3, LLaMA) with single hyperparameter settings per RL method; other architectures, scales beyond 14B, or alternative KL coefficients may produce different decoupling behaviors.\nCED scoring operates at the document level using an external NLI model (DeBERTa-v3); sensitivity to other NLI model families is unexplored, and sentence-level CED filtering disrupts co-reference chains (Evi-F1 $-$7.7pp).",
    "newString": "We evaluate on two datasets (DocRED, Re-DocRED), both derived from Wikipedia; cross-domain evaluation is constrained by the absence of evidence-annotated DocRE datasets beyond Wikipedia, a limitation shared by all prior evidence-aware work (SAIS, DREEAM, EIDER).\nEvidence tax patterns span two model families (Qwen3, LLaMA) with single hyperparameter settings per RL method; other architectures, scales beyond 14B, or alternative KL coefficients may produce different decoupling behaviors.\nCED scoring operates at the document level using an external NLI model (DeBERTa-v3); sensitivity to other NLI model families is unexplored, and sentence-level CED filtering disrupts co-reference chains (Evi-F1 $-$7.7pp).\nOur explanation for why RSFT avoids decoupling---that discrete selection cannot exploit the extraction--evidence trade-off---is structural rather than empirical. We have not isolated whether RSFT's advantage stems primarily from removing low-quality training examples, from implicit data augmentation through candidate diversity, or from both. Disentangling these factors would require controlled experiments with fixed training data quality but varied selection mechanisms.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Limitations}\n\nWe evaluate on two datasets (DocRED, Re-DocRED), both derived from Wikipedia; cross-domain evaluation is constrained by the absence of evidence-annotated DocRE datasets beyond Wikipedia, a limitation shared by all prior evidence-aware work (SAIS, DREEAM, EIDER).\nEvidence tax patterns span two model families (Qwen3, LLaMA) with single hyperparameter settings per RL method; other architectures, scales beyond 14B, or alternative KL coefficients may produce different decoupling behaviors.\nCED scoring operates at the document level using an external NLI model (DeBERTa-v3); sensitivity to other NLI model families is unexplored, and sentence-level CED filtering disrupts co-reference chains (Evi-F1 $-$7.7pp).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 3,
        "oldLines": 3,
        "newStart": 3,
        "newLines": 4,
        "lines": [
          " We evaluate on two datasets (DocRED, Re-DocRED), both derived from Wikipedia; cross-domain evaluation is constrained by the absence of evidence-annotated DocRE datasets beyond Wikipedia, a limitation shared by all prior evidence-aware work (SAIS, DREEAM, EIDER).",
          " Evidence tax patterns span two model families (Qwen3, LLaMA) with single hyperparameter settings per RL method; other architectures, scales beyond 14B, or alternative KL coefficients may produce different decoupling behaviors.",
          " CED scoring operates at the document level using an external NLI model (DeBERTa-v3); sensitivity to other NLI model families is unexplored, and sentence-level CED filtering disrupts co-reference chains (Evi-F1 $-$7.7pp).",
          "+Our explanation for why RSFT avoids decoupling---that discrete selection cannot exploit the extraction--evidence trade-off---is structural rather than empirical. We have not isolated whether RSFT's advantage stems primarily from removing low-quality training examples, from implicit data augmentation through candidate diversity, or from both. Disentangling these factors would require controlled experiments with fixed training data quality but varied selection mechanisms."
        ]
      }
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Q1LB8nsqnbsaV1voME5d4r`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	Document-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires reasoning across sentence boundaries to identify relations between entity pairs.
5	DocRED \citep{yao2019docred} established the first large-scale benchmark for this task, catalyzing a line of encoder-based methods that treat DocRE as classification over entity pair representations.
6	ATLOP \citep{zhou2021atlop} introduced adaptive thresholding with localized context pooling, and graph-based methods such as GAIN \citep{zeng2020gain} model inter-sentence reasoning through heterogeneous mention-level and entity-level graphs.
7	A parallel thread incorporates sentence-level evidence into these architectures.
8	SAIS \citep{xiao2022sais} introduced evidence supervision as an auxiliary training signal and showed that joint evidence prediction improves extraction through data augmentation and ensemble inference.
9	DREEAM \citep{ma2023dreeam} extended this approach with dynamic memory-based evidence retrieval that guides attention to supporting sentences.
10	EIDER \citep{xie2022eider} jointly trains an evidence extractor and fuses evidence representations into the relation classifier.
11	In all three systems, evidence identification is binary sentence classification over a fixed candidate set, a formulation that adds no output complexity.
12	Recent work has recast DocRE for large language models.
13	AutoRE \citep{autore2024} fine-tunes LLMs with QLoRA using a relation-head-facts decomposition; \citet{xu2024lmrc} frame extraction as multi-choice reading comprehension; RelPrior \citep{wang2025relprior} uses relation priors to guide LLM-based extraction; GLiDRE \citep{zhang2025glidre} presents a generalist lightweight approach; Ru-LLM \citep{wang2025rullm} incorporates logical rule constraints; and \citet{chen2025docksrag} combine retrieval-augmented generation with structured knowledge.
14	These methods match or surpass encoder baselines but produce bare triplets without evidence. Among LLM-based generative methods at the 8B scale, our RSFT model (54.33 Ign-F1 on DocRED dev) outperforms AutoRE (52.14\footnote{AutoRE Ign-F1 is from the LLaMA3-8B reproduction by \citet{wang2025relprior}; the original paper reports only F1.}) and is slightly below RelPrior ($-$1.17pp), while being the only method that additionally provides sentence-level evidence grounding.
15	The critical distinction lies in computational nature: in SAIS and DREEAM, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder without increasing output length; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires additional output tokens per triple, imposing costs that depend on model capacity and optimization strategy.
16	Whether this cost is inevitable or can be mitigated is the central question of our work.
17	
18	Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) \citep{shao2024deepseekmath} replaces the value network of PPO \citep{schulman2017ppo} by computing advantages within groups.
19	Subsequent work addresses failure modes: DAPO \citep{yu2025dapo} prevents entropy collapse, Dr.\ GRPO \citep{liu2025drgrpo} corrects length bias, $\mu$-GRPO \citep{tian2026mugrpo} improves off-policy efficiency, Tree-GRPO \citep{ji2026treegrpo} extends GRPO to agentic settings, and multi-objective variants \citep{liu2026gdpo,ito2025mogrpo} handle competing reward signals.
20	Applications to structured IE include R1-RE \citep{dai2025r1re} and REAR \citep{wang2025rear}.
21	Direct Preference Optimization \citep{rafailov2023direct} reformulates Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback \citep[RLHF;][]{ouyang2022instructgpt} as implicit reward maximization; KTO \citep{ethayarajh2024kto} further simplifies alignment by learning from binary desirability signals without paired preferences.
22	Our decoupling spectrum is distinct from catastrophic forgetting in RL alignment \citep{jin2025rl_panacea}, where models lose pre-trained capabilities across tasks; in our setting, extraction and evidence compete \emph{within} a single task, resembling negative transfer in multi-task learning \citep{ruder2017overview,standley2020tasks,yu2020gradient}, amplified by QLoRA's \citep{dettmers2023qlora} constrained parameter space and capacity-dependent scaling dynamics \citep{hoffmann2022chinchilla,gao2023scaling,moskovitz2024constrained,coste2024reward}.
23	RSFT avoids this intra-task competition by operating on discrete outputs rather than gradients, extending the sample-and-select principle \citep{wang2023selfconsistency} to structured IE with joint quality scoring.
24	
25	Evidence faithfulness evaluation has received increasing attention: \citet{laban2022summac} re-visit NLI-based models for summarization inconsistency detection, \citet{li2025drift} propose dual-reward inference for faithful rationale generation, \citet{li2025srvf} use supervised rationale verification with contrastive feedback for relation extraction, \citet{zhang2024infuse} introduce fine-grained NLI-based faithfulness metrics for summarization, and EvidenceRL \citep{tamo2026evidencerl} reinforces evidence consistency through GRPO-based optimization with grounding rewards.
26	Our CED scoring builds on similar NLI-based principles but targets evidence-relation entailment rather than free-form rationales.
27	
28	\citet{zelikman2022star} introduced STaR for iterative self-improvement; \citet{yuan2023rft} demonstrate the scaling properties of rejection sampling fine-tuning; \citet{dong2023raft} generalize with reward-model-based sample filtering; and recent analysis shows rejection sampling implicitly performs PPO-like optimization \citep{tang2025rsftppo}. Extensions include RIFT \citep{liu2026rift}, which repurposes rejected samples, and TrajFusion \citep{deng2026trajfusion}, which fuses trajectories beyond simple selection.
29	Our RSFT is a single-round variant applied to evidence-grounded DocRE with CED/NLI scoring as a domain-specific quality filter.
30	Because RSFT selects complete outputs rather than differentiating through a reward signal, it cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off that gradient methods discover, inherently preserving joint quality.
31	
```

</details>

```json
  "old_string": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\nDocument-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires reasoning across sentence boundaries to identify relations between entity pairs.\nDocRED \\citep{yao2019docred} established the first large-scale benchmark for this task, catalyzing a line of encoder-based methods that treat DocRE as classification over entity pair representations.\nATLOP \\citep{zhou2021atlop} introduced adaptive thresholding with localized context pooling, and graph-based methods such as GAIN \\citep{zeng2020gain} model inter-sentence reasoning through heterogeneous mention-level and entity-level graphs.\nA parallel thread incorporates sentence-level evidence into these architectures.\nSAIS \\citep{xiao2022sais} introduced evidence supervision as an auxiliary training signal and showed that joint evidence prediction improves extraction through data augmentation and ensemble inference.\nDREEAM \\citep{ma2023dreeam} extended this approach with dynamic memory-based evidence retrieval that guides attention to supporting sentences.\nEIDER \\citep{xie2022eider} jointly trains an evidence extractor and fuses evidence representations into the relation classifier.\nIn all three systems, evidence identification is binary sentence classification over a fixed candidate set, a formulation that adds no output complexity.\nRecent work has recast DocRE for large language models.\nAutoRE \\citep{autore2024} fine-tunes LLMs with QLoRA using a relation-head-facts decomposition; \\citet{xu2024lmrc} frame extraction as multi-choice reading comprehension; RelPrior \\citep{wang2025relprior} uses relation priors to guide LLM-based extraction; GLiDRE \\citep{zhang2025glidre} presents a generalist lightweight approach; Ru-LLM \\citep{wang2025rullm} incorporates logical rule constraints; and \\citet{chen2025docksrag} combine retrieval-augmented generation with structured knowledge.\nThese methods match or surpass encoder baselines but produce bare triplets without evidence. Among LLM-based generative methods at the 8B scale, our RSFT model (54.33 Ign-F1 on DocRED dev) outperforms AutoRE (52.14\\footnote{AutoRE Ign-F1 is from the LLaMA3-8B reproduction by \\citet{wang2025relprior}; the original paper reports only F1.}) and is slightly below RelPrior ($-$1.17pp), while being the only method that additionally provides sentence-level evidence grounding.\nThe critical distinction lies in computational nature: in SAIS and DREEAM, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder without increasing output length; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires additional output tokens per triple, imposing costs that depend on model capacity and optimization strategy.\nWhether this cost is inevitable or can be mitigated is the central question of our work.\n\nGroup Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) \\citep{shao2024deepseekmath} replaces the value network of PPO \\citep{schulman2017ppo} by computing advantages within groups.\nSubsequent work addresses failure modes: DAPO \\citep{yu2025dapo} prevents entropy collapse, Dr.\\ GRPO \\citep{liu2025drgrpo} corrects length bias, $\\mu$-GRPO \\citep{tian2026mugrpo} improves off-policy efficiency, Tree-GRPO \\citep{ji2026treegrpo} extends GRPO to agentic settings, and multi-objective variants \\citep{liu2026gdpo,ito2025mogrpo} handle competing reward signals.\nApplications to structured IE include R1-RE \\citep{dai2025r1re} and REAR \\citep{wang2025rear}.\nDirect Preference Optimization \\citep{rafailov2023direct} reformulates Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback \\citep[RLHF;][]{ouyang2022instructgpt} as implicit reward maximization; KTO \\citep{ethayarajh2024kto} further simplifies alignment by learning from binary desirability signals without paired preferences.\nOur decoupling spectrum is distinct from catastrophic forgetting in RL alignment \\citep{jin2025rl_panacea}, where models lose pre-trained capabilities across tasks; in our setting, extraction and evidence compete \\emph{within} a single task, resembling negative transfer in multi-task learning \\citep{ruder2017overview,standley2020tasks,yu2020gradient}, amplified by QLoRA's \\citep{dettmers2023qlora} constrained parameter space and capacity-dependent scaling dynamics \\citep{hoffmann2022chinchilla,gao2023scaling,moskovitz2024constrained,coste2024reward}.\nRSFT avoids this intra-task competition by operating on discrete outputs rather than gradients, extending the sample-and-select principle \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency} to structured IE with joint quality scoring.\n\nEvidence faithfulness evaluation has received increasing attention: \\citet{laban2022summac} re-visit NLI-based models for summarization inconsistency detection, \\citet{li2025drift} propose dual-reward inference for faithful rationale generation, \\citet{li2025srvf} use supervised rationale verification with contrastive feedback for relation extraction, \\citet{zhang2024infuse} introduce fine-grained NLI-based faithfulness metrics for summarization, and EvidenceRL \\citep{tamo2026evidencerl} reinforces evidence consistency through GRPO-based optimization with grounding rewards.\nOur CED scoring builds on similar NLI-based principles but targets evidence-relation entailment rather than free-form rationales.\n\n\\citet{zelikman2022star} introduced STaR for iterative self-improvement; \\citet{yuan2023rft} demonstrate the scaling properties of rejection sampling fine-tuning; \\citet{dong2023raft} generalize with reward-model-based sample filtering; and recent analysis shows rejection sampling implicitly performs PPO-like optimization \\citep{tang2025rsftppo}. Extensions include RIFT \\citep{liu2026rift}, which repurposes rejected samples, and TrajFusion \\citep{deng2026trajfusion}, which fuses trajectories beyond simple selection.\nOur RSFT is a single-round variant applied to evidence-grounded DocRE with CED/NLI scoring as a domain-specific quality filter.\nBecause RSFT selects complete outputs rather than differentiating through a reward signal, it cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off that gradient methods discover, inherently preserving joint quality.",
  "new_string": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\subsection{Document-Level Relation Extraction}\n\\label{sec:rw_docre}\n\nDocument-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires reasoning across sentence boundaries to identify relations between entity pairs.\nDocRED \\citep{yao2019docred} established the first large-scale benchmark for this task, catalyzing a line of encoder-based methods that treat DocRE as classification over entity pair representations.\nATLOP \\citep{zhou2021atlop} introduced adaptive thresholding with localized context pooling, and graph-based methods such as GAIN \\citep{zeng2020gain} model inter-sentence reasoning through heterogeneous mention-level and entity-level graphs.\nA parallel thread incorporates sentence-level evidence into these architectures.\nSAIS \\citep{xiao2022sais} introduced evidence supervision as an auxiliary training signal and showed that joint evidence prediction improves extraction through data augmentation and ensemble inference.\nDREEAM \\citep{ma2023dreeam} extended this approach with dynamic memory-based evidence retrieval that guides attention to supporting sentences.\nEIDER \\citep{xie2022eider} jointly trains an evidence extractor and fuses evidence representations into the relation classifier.\nIn all three systems, evidence identification is binary sentence classification over a fixed candidate set, a formulation that adds no output complexity.\nRecent work has recast DocRE for large language models.\nAutoRE \\citep{autore2024} fine-tunes LLMs with QLoRA using a relation-head-facts decomposition; \\citet{xu2024lmrc} frame extraction as multi-choice reading comprehension; RelPrior \\citep{wang2025relprior} uses relation priors to guide LLM-based extraction; GLiDRE \\citep{zhang2025glidre} presents a generalist lightweight approach; the original paper reports only F1.}) and is slightly below RelPrior ($-$1.17pp), while being the only method that additionally provides sentence-level evidence grounding.\nThe critical distinction lies in computational nature: in SAIS and DREEAM, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder without increasing output length; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires additional output tokens per triple, imposing costs that depend on model capacity and optimization strategy.\nWhether this cost is inevitable or can be mitigated is the central question of our work.\n\n\\subsection{Reinforcement Learning for Structured Generation}\n\\label{sec:rw_rl}\n\nGroup Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) \\citep{shao2024deepseekmath} replaces the value network of PPO \\citep{schulman2017ppo} by computing advantages within groups.\nSubsequent work addresses failure modes: DAPO \\citep{yu2025dapo} prevents entropy collapse, Dr.\\ GRPO \\citep{liu2025drgrpo} corrects length bias, $\\mu$-GRPO \\citep{tian2026mugrpo} improves off-policy efficiency, Tree-GRPO \\citep{ji2026treegrpo} extends GRPO to agentic settings, and multi-objective variants \\citep{liu2026gdpo,ito2025mogrpo} handle competing reward signals.\nApplications to structured IE include R1-RE \\citep{dai2025r1re} and REAR \\citep{wang2025rear}.\nDirect Preference Optimization \\citep{rafailov2023direct} reformulates Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback \\citep[RLHF;][]{ouyang2022instructgpt} as implicit reward maximization; in our setting, extraction and evidence compete \\emph{within} a single task, resembling negative transfer in multi-task learning \\citep{ruder2017overview,standley2020tasks,yu2020gradient}, amplified by QLoRA's \\citep{dettmers2023qlora} constrained parameter space and capacity-dependent scaling dynamics \\citep{hoffmann2022chinchilla,gao2023scaling,moskovitz2024constrained,coste2024reward}.\n\n\\subsection{Evidence Faithfulness and Rejection Sampling}\n\\label{sec:rw_faithfulness}\n\nEvidence faithfulness evaluation has received increasing attention: \\citet{laban2022summac} re-visit NLI-based models for summarization inconsistency detection, \\citet{li2025drift} propose dual-reward inference for faithful rationale generation, \\citet{li2025srvf} use supervised rationale verification with contrastive feedback for relation extraction, \\citet{zhang2024infuse} introduce fine-grained NLI-based faithfulness metrics for summarization, and EvidenceRL \\citep{tamo2026evidencerl} reinforces evidence consistency through GRPO-based optimization with grounding rewards.\nOur CED scoring builds on similar NLI-based principles but targets evidence-relation entailment rather than free-form rationales.\n\n\\citet{zelikman2022star} introduced STaR for iterative self-improvement; \\citet{yuan2023rft} demonstrate the scaling properties of rejection sampling fine-tuning; \\citet{dong2023raft} generalize with reward-model-based sample filtering; and recent analysis shows rejection sampling implicitly performs PPO-like optimization \\citep{tang2025rsftppo}. Extensions include RIFT \\citep{liu2026rift}, which repurposes rejected samples, and TrajFusion \\citep{deng2026trajfusion}, which fuses trajectories beyond simple selection.\nRSFT avoids intra-task competition by operating on discrete outputs rather than gradients, extending the sample-and-select principle \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency} to structured IE with joint quality scoring.\nBecause RSFT selects complete outputs rather than differentiating through a reward signal, it cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off that gradient methods discover, inherently preserving joint quality."
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\nDocument-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires reasoning across sentence boundaries to identify relations between entity pairs.\nDocRED \\citep{yao2019docred} established the first large-scale benchmark for this task, catalyzing a line of encoder-based methods that treat DocRE as classification over entity pair representations.\nATLOP \\citep{zhou2021atlop} introduced adaptive thresholding with localized context pooling, and graph-based methods such as GAIN \\citep{zeng2020gain} model inter-sentence reasoning through heterogeneous mention-level and entity-level graphs.\nA parallel thread incorporates sentence-level evidence into these architectures.\nSAIS \\citep{xiao2022sais} introduced evidence supervision as an auxiliary training signal and showed that joint evidence prediction improves extraction through data augmentation and ensemble inference.\nDREEAM \\citep{ma2023dreeam} extended this approach with dynamic memory-based evidence retrieval that guides attention to supporting sentences.\nEIDER \\citep{xie2022eider} jointly trains an evidence extractor and fuses evidence representations into the relation classifier.\nIn all three systems, evidence identification is binary sentence classification over a fixed candidate set, a formulation that adds no output complexity.\nRecent work has recast DocRE for large language models.\nAutoRE \\citep{autore2024} fine-tunes LLMs with QLoRA using a relation-head-facts decomposition; \\citet{xu2024lmrc} frame extraction as multi-choice reading comprehension; RelPrior \\citep{wang2025relprior} uses relation priors to guide LLM-based extraction; GLiDRE \\citep{zhang2025glidre} presents a generalist lightweight approach; Ru-LLM \\citep{wang2025rullm} incorporates logical rule constraints; and \\citet{chen2025docksrag} combine retrieval-augmented generation with structured knowledge.\nThese methods match or surpass encoder baselines but produce bare triplets without evidence. Among LLM-based generative methods at the 8B scale, our RSFT model (54.33 Ign-F1 on DocRED dev) outperforms AutoRE (52.14\\footnote{AutoRE Ign-F1 is from the LLaMA3-8B reproduction by \\citet{wang2025relprior}; the original paper reports only F1.}) and is slightly below RelPrior ($-$1.17pp), while being the only method that additionally provides sentence-level evidence grounding.\nThe critical distinction lies in computational nature: in SAIS and DREEAM, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder without increasing output length; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires additional output tokens per triple, imposing costs that depend on model capacity and optimization strategy.\nWhether this cost is inevitable or can be mitigated is the central question of our work.\n\nGroup Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) \\citep{shao2024deepseekmath} replaces the value network of PPO \\citep{schulman2017ppo} by computing advantages within groups.\nSubsequent work addresses failure modes: DAPO \\citep{yu2025dapo} prevents entropy collapse, Dr.\\ GRPO \\citep{liu2025drgrpo} corrects length bias, $\\mu$-GRPO \\citep{tian2026mugrpo} improves off-policy efficiency, Tree-GRPO \\citep{ji2026treegrpo} extends GRPO to agentic settings, and multi-objective variants \\citep{liu2026gdpo,ito2025mogrpo} handle competing reward signals.\nApplications to structured IE include R1-RE \\citep{dai2025r1re} and REAR \\citep{wang2025rear}.\nDirect Preference Optimization \\citep{rafailov2023direct} reformulates Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback \\citep[RLHF;][]{ouyang2022instructgpt} as implicit reward maximization; KTO \\citep{ethayarajh2024kto} further simplifies alignment by learning from binary desirability signals without paired preferences.\nOur decoupling spectrum is distinct from catastrophic forgetting in RL alignment \\citep{jin2025rl_panacea}, where models lose pre-trained capabilities across tasks; in our setting, extraction and evidence compete \\emph{within} a single task, resembling negative transfer in multi-task learning \\citep{ruder2017overview,standley2020tasks,yu2020gradient}, amplified by QLoRA's \\citep{dettmers2023qlora} constrained parameter space and capacity-dependent scaling dynamics \\citep{hoffmann2022chinchilla,gao2023scaling,moskovitz2024constrained,coste2024reward}.\nRSFT avoids this intra-task competition by operating on discrete outputs rather than gradients, extending the sample-and-select principle \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency} to structured IE with joint quality scoring.\n\nEvidence faithfulness evaluation has received increasing attention: \\citet{laban2022summac} re-visit NLI-based models for summarization inconsistency detection, \\citet{li2025drift} propose dual-reward inference for faithful rationale generation, \\citet{li2025srvf} use supervised rationale verification with contrastive feedback for relation extraction, \\citet{zhang2024infuse} introduce fine-grained NLI-based faithfulness metrics for summarization, and EvidenceRL \\citep{tamo2026evidencerl} reinforces evidence consistency through GRPO-based optimization with grounding rewards.\nOur CED scoring builds on similar NLI-based principles but targets evidence-relation entailment rather than free-form rationales.\n\n\\citet{zelikman2022star} introduced STaR for iterative self-improvement; \\citet{yuan2023rft} demonstrate the scaling properties of rejection sampling fine-tuning; \\citet{dong2023raft} generalize with reward-model-based sample filtering; and recent analysis shows rejection sampling implicitly performs PPO-like optimization \\citep{tang2025rsftppo}. Extensions include RIFT \\citep{liu2026rift}, which repurposes rejected samples, and TrajFusion \\citep{deng2026trajfusion}, which fuses trajectories beyond simple selection.\nOur RSFT is a single-round variant applied to evidence-grounded DocRE with CED/NLI scoring as a domain-specific quality filter.\nBecause RSFT selects complete outputs rather than differentiating through a reward signal, it cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off that gradient methods discover, inherently preserving joint quality.\n",
```

```text
36	
37	\subsection{Evidence-Extraction Decoupling Spectrum}
38	\label{sec:spectrum}
39	
40	Because evidence and extraction share gradient capacity, different optimization paradigms handle this competition in qualitatively different ways. We organize the outcomes along a \emph{decoupling spectrum} from catastrophic collapse to no decoupling (Figure~\ref{fig:spectrum}; full data in Table~\ref{tab:spectrum}, Appendix; training dynamics in Figure~\ref{fig:grpo_dynamics}, Appendix).
41	
42	\paragraph{GRPO.}
43	Under QLoRA 4-bit quantization ($G{=}4$), GRPO drives Rel-F1 to 1.38 while Evi-F1 rises to 85.04 (EDCR 0.9876): quantization-constrained precision limits the effective parameter space, so the reward signal preferentially optimizes the easier sub-task~\citep{gao2023scaling}, resembling the quantized-actor divergence of \citet{li2026qurl}. Full-precision bf16 ($G{=}8$) avoids catastrophe (Rel-F1 45.49, EDCR 0.6318), confirming quantization precision rather than group size as the primary factor.
44	
45	\paragraph{DPO and RSFT.}
46	DPO exhibits substantial extraction degradation: Rel-F1 drops from 33.37 (1 epoch) to 31.45 (3 epochs) while Evi-F1 rises (80.90$\to$84.84). EDCR is non-monotonic (0.8123$\to$0.7503): despite worsening extraction, evidence selectivity improves with continued training, partially reducing the decoupling. RSFT maintains both capabilities simultaneously (Rel-F1 45.80, Evi-F1 80.96, EDCR 0.6487) because discrete output selection cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off.
47	
48	\begin{figure}[t]
49	\centering
50	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_spectrum.pdf}
51	\caption{Decoupling spectrum. Each method is plotted by Rel-F1 (x-axis) and Evi-F1 (y-axis). Arrows indicate training progression for DPO and GRPO bf16. Decoupled methods drift toward the upper-left.}
52	\label{fig:spectrum}
53	\end{figure}
54	
55	
56	\subsection{Scale-Dependent Evidence Tax}
57	\label{sec:scaling}
58	
59	The evidence tax, defined as Ign-F1(no-evidence) $-$ Ign-F1(with-evidence) on the development set, is scale-dependent. Under matched evaluation conditions (\texttt{max\_new\_tokens}$=$2048, format-compliant intersection; Appendix~\ref{sec:training_tax}), the training-time tax reveals a within-family capacity threshold: evidence generation benefits 1.7B ($-$5.63pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:training_tax}), where it also halves the output duplication rate (60.5\%$\to$33.0\%; Table~\ref{tab:mechanistic} in Appendix~\ref{sec:fair_subset}), but is approximately neutral at $\geq$4B (+0.25$\pm$0.47pp at 4B, $n{=}3$; +0.04pp at 8B; +0.03pp at 14B). Table~\ref{tab:scaling} and Figure~\ref{fig:evidence_tax} present RSFT performance and inference-time evidence tax across five models (Figure~\ref{fig:scaling} in Appendix). Evidence training also affects SFT seed variance in a scale-dependent manner: at 4B and 8B, with-evidence models show lower cross-seed spread than no-evidence models (25$\times$ and 1.4$\times$ compression, respectively), but at 1.7B the pattern reverses---with-evidence variance exceeds no-evidence variance, likely amplified by extreme truncation rates at this scale (Appendix~\ref{sec:training_tax}).
60
```

```text
100	
101	\subsection{Evidence Strategy Comparison}
102	\label{sec:evidence_strategy}
103	
104	We compare joint generation against post-hoc BM25 retrieval (entity-constrained, $k{=}2$), using Evi-F1 (joint) with the DREEAM-protocol denominator~\citep{ma2023dreeam}. Post-hoc retrieval achieves higher Rel-F1 (48.76) by avoiding the evidence tax but lower Evi-F1 (joint) of 34.7 (EDCR 0.766). RSFT CED reaches 36.1 (EDCR 0.6487), a 1.4-point evidence advantage. CED reranking at inference (best-of-$N{=}8$ by CED score) achieves Rel-F1 48.64 and Evi-F1 81.88 (TP-only), approaching no-evidence extraction quality while preserving evidence.
105	
106	
107	\subsection{Generalization}
108	\label{sec:generalization}
109	
110	Cross-architecture validation on LLaMA-3.1-8B (Table~\ref{tab:scaling}) shows the largest RSFT gain (+5.45pp) with complete pairwise seed separation (\S\ref{sec:scaling}). The RSFT improvement transfers across datasets: on Re-DocRED~\citep{tan2022redocred}, RSFT achieves 41.45 Ign-F1 (+4.86pp over SFT 36.59), matching the DocRED gain (+5.08pp). Our best joint Evi-F1 (36.1, DREEAM-protocol) falls below supervised DREEAM (54.15\%~\citep{ma2023dreeam}), a gap driven by extraction performance rather than evidence quality (TP-only Evi-F1 exceeds 80\%). On the Re-DocRED test set, RSFT Ign-F1 drops approximately 20pp relative to dev across all evaluated scales (e.g., 14B: 58.5$\to$38.5), attributable to 2.82$\times$ higher annotation density in test (34.7 vs.\ 12.3 gold triples per document); model ranking is perfectly preserved (Spearman $\rho{=}1.0$; Table~\ref{tab:test_results} in Appendix). Full results appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:generalization_full}.
111	
112	
113	\subsection{Discussion}
114	\label{sec:analysis}
115	
116	EDCR (\S\ref{sec:edcr}) validates as a diagnostic at both cross-method ($\rho = -0.96$, $n = 7$ unique methods, $p < 0.001$; Appendix~\ref{sec:edcr_per_relation}) and per-relation levels ($\rho = -0.79$, $n = 76$, $p < 0.001$; Appendix~\ref{sec:edcr_per_relation}), the latter orthogonal to Evi-F1 ($\rho = -0.06$); partial correlations controlling for shared components are virtually unchanged ($\rho = -0.9617$ vs.\ $-0.9643$). CED and flat-NLI produce comparable RSFT results (Rel-F1 45.63 vs.\ 45.61); stricter selection ($k{=}1$) outperforms $k{=}3$ by 1.02 Ign-F1 despite fewer examples (Appendix~\ref{sec:ablation_full}). RSFT reduces truncation FN, hallucinated FP, and duplicates by 6--22\% across scales, but evidence quality is unchanged ($\Delta < 0.4$pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:error_analysis}): RSFT improves \emph{what} the model extracts but not \emph{how well} it grounds extractions---concrete evidence citation comparisons in Appendix~\ref{sec:qualitative} illustrate this pattern. Inference overhead analysis (Appendix~\ref{sec:inference_efficiency}) reveals that evidence generation adds 16--24\% wall-clock time at 4B--14B scale but paradoxically reduces inference time at 1.7B by 13.6\%, as the evidence field constrains output structure and reduces prediction count by 1.75$\times$.
117	
```

```text
345	{\raggedright\footnotesize $^\ddagger$Three-seed mean. DPO at @2048 (matched); others at @1024. Severity reflects training trajectory stability, not final EDCR.\par}
346	\end{table}
347	
348	
349	\section{GRPO Training Dynamics}
350	\label{sec:grpo_dynamics}
351	
352	Figure~\ref{fig:grpo_dynamics} traces Rel-F1, Evi-F1, and EDCR across training checkpoints for two GRPO configurations, illustrating how catastrophic decoupling emerges under constrained optimization (G=4 QLoRA) but not under full-precision training (G=8 bf16).
353	
354	\begin{figure*}[t]
355	\centering
356	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig_grpo_dynamics.pdf}
357	\caption{Training dynamics of GRPO under different configurations. (a)~Relation F1 across checkpoints: G=4 QLoRA suffers catastrophic collapse at step~10, while G=8 bf16 remains stable. (b)~Evidence F1 stays intact for G=8 bf16, confirming the generation mechanism itself does not fail. (c)~Evidence-Decoupling Ratio (EDCR) reveals near-total decoupling in G=4 QLoRA (extraction collapses to Rel-F1 1.4), while G=8 bf16 maintains healthy ratios.}
358	\label{fig:grpo_dynamics}
359	\end{figure*}
360	
361	
362	\section{Error Analysis: SFT vs.\ RSFT}
363	\label{sec:error_analysis}
364	
365	Table~\ref{tab:error_analysis} decomposes the extraction improvements from RSFT into error categories across three scales. Three mechanisms drive the gains consistently:
366	
367	\paragraph{Truncation false negatives.} SFT models frequently hit the generation length limit, producing incomplete outputs that miss triples. RSFT training on compact, high-quality outputs reduces this truncation rate. The effect is strongest at 1.7B ($-841$ truncation FN, $-22$\%), where the SFT model's output length is least controlled.
368	
369	\paragraph{Hallucinated false positives.} CED-based filtering removes training examples with unfaithful predictions, reducing the rate of hallucinated triples at inference. LLaMA benefits most ($-593$ hallucinated FP, $-11.8$\%), suggesting cross-architecture variation in hallucination susceptibility.
370	
371	\paragraph{Duplicate predictions.} RSFT training data, selected for quality, contains fewer repetitive patterns. This propagates to inference, reducing duplicate triples by $11$--$20$\% across scales, with 1.7B showing the largest reduction ($-20.2$\%).
372	
373	\paragraph{Evidence quality unchanged.} Despite substantial extraction improvements, evidence exact-match F1 changes by less than 0.4pp at every scale. RSFT selects for joint extraction-evidence quality, but the evidence signal in CED scoring does not translate into measurable evidence improvement. This suggests that evidence grounding requires targeted optimization (e.g., evidence-specific rewards in RL) rather than data selection alone.
374	
375	\paragraph{Residual difficulty.} The proportion of ``other'' false negatives (triples missed for reasons beyond truncation or formatting) increases by 13--15\% in relative terms after RSFT. As easy errors (truncation, duplication) are eliminated, the remaining error distribution shifts toward harder cases: cross-sentence reasoning, implicit relations, and long-tail entity pairs. The relation types with the largest RSFT gains (participant, conflict, league, screenwriter, operator, contains\_administrative\_territory) involve cross-sentence reasoning or long-tail entities, consistent with RSFT's selection pressure toward diverse, high-quality outputs.
376	
377	\begin{table}[t]
378	\centering
379	\caption{Error pattern changes from SFT to RSFT across scales. $\Delta$\% shows relative change. Truncation FN, hallucinated FP, and duplicates all decrease; evidence quality and residual FN difficulty are unchanged.}
380	\label{tab:error_analysis}
381	\small
382	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
383	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
384	\toprule
385	\textbf{Error Category} & \textbf{Direction} & \textbf{1.7B} & \textbf{4B} & \textbf{8B / LLaMA} \\
386	\midrule
387	Truncation FN & $\downarrow$ & $-$22\% & $-$16\% & $-$18\% \\
388	Hallucinated FP & $\downarrow$ & $-$8\% & $-$6\% & $-$12\% \\
389	Duplicates & $\downarrow$ & $-$20\% & $-$11\% & $-$14\% \\
390	Evidence (exact) & $\approx$ & $<$0.4pp & $<$0.4pp & $<$0.4pp \\
391	FN: other & $\uparrow$ & +13\% & +15\% & +14\% \\
392	\bottomrule
393	\end{tabular}%
394	}
```

```text
394	}
395	\end{table}
396	
397	
398	\section{Per-Relation EDCR Distribution}
399	\label{sec:edcr_per_relation}
400	
401	Computing EDCR for each of the 76 relation types with $\geq$5 evaluation samples (from the RSFT CED s42 model) reveals that per-relation EDCR correlates strongly with per-relation Rel-F1 ($\rho = -0.79$, $n = 76$, $p < 0.001$), confirming its utility as a difficulty indicator. Filtering to types with $\geq$5 predictions strengthens the correlation ($\rho = -0.85$, $n = 74$, $p < 0.001$). Per-relation EDCR is statistically independent of Evi-F1 ($\rho = -0.06$, $p = 0.58$), and Evi-F1 itself shows no association with Rel-F1 ($\rho = -0.01$, $p = 0.91$). These null correlations establish that evidence quality and extraction ability are fully decoupled across relation types. Extreme cases illustrate the diagnostic power: \texttt{date\_of\_birth} has EDCR $= 0.118$ (highly faithful, with extraction and evidence tightly coupled), while \texttt{head\_of\_government} reaches $0.956$ (highly decoupled). This per-relation analysis avoids the shared-component concern present in cross-method EDCR-vs-Rel-F1 correlation, providing independent validation of EDCR's diagnostic utility. A permutation test (10,000 iterations) on the per-relation correlation yields $p < 0.0001$, confirming that the association is not an artifact of the shared TP component.
402	
403	\paragraph{Partial correlations controlling for TP.}
404	Because EDCR shares TP (true positives) in both numerator and denominator with Rel-F1, we report partial correlations controlling for Evi-Recall$_\text{TP}$. At the cross-method level ($n = 17$ seed-specific configurations), the partial Spearman $\rho = -0.8541$ ($p = 0.000025$), virtually unchanged from the raw $\rho = -0.8554$ (0.15\% reduction). Removing the GRPO QLoRA outlier yields partial $\rho = -0.8681$ ($n = 16$, $p = 0.000027$). Aggregating to $n = 7$ unique methods: raw $\rho = -0.9643$, partial $\rho = -0.9617$. These results confirm that the EDCR--Rel-F1 association is not driven by the shared TP component. Note that seed-level configurations ($n = 17$) are not fully independent; the $n = 7$ unique-method analysis provides a conservative estimate.
405	
406	\paragraph{Similar-TP, different-EDCR pairs.}
407	To further validate that EDCR captures information beyond what TP alone provides, we identify configuration pairs with similar Evi-Recall$_\text{TP}$ ($|\Delta| < 5$pp) but substantially different EDCR ($|\Delta| > 5$pp). Forty-eight such pairs exist across all configurations. The most illustrative: DPO-CED 1-epoch (Evi-Recall$_\text{TP} = 0.833$) vs.\ RSFT CED LLaMA s43 (Evi-Recall$_\text{TP} = 0.829$) differ by only 2.4pp in TP-conditioned evidence recall but 13.6pp in EDCR, reflecting the substantial extraction gap between these methods. This pattern demonstrates that EDCR captures extraction-evidence alignment that aggregate TP metrics miss.
408	
409	\begin{figure}[t]
410	\centering
411	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig_edcr_relations.pdf}
412	\caption{Per-relation EDCR distribution across all 93 relation types observed in the development set (of 96 defined in DocRED). The correlation analysis in the text uses the 76 types with $\geq$5 samples for statistical reliability. Red bars indicate EDCR $\geq$ 0.70 (severe decoupling), green bars indicate EDCR $<$ 0.30 (healthy evidence grounding). Seven types exhibit EDCR = 1.0.}
413	\label{fig:edcr_relations}
414	\end{figure}
415	
416	
417	\section{Qualitative Analysis}
418	\label{sec:qualitative}
419	
420	We present representative examples illustrating three phenomena: evidence quality differences between SFT and RSFT, GRPO format gaming under catastrophic collapse, and per-relation EDCR diagnostics.
421	
422	\paragraph{Case 1: Evidence quality.}
423	Table~\ref{tab:case_evidence} compares evidence citations from SFT and RSFT on three documents. SFT tends to cite many sentences indiscriminately, including distractors unrelated to the target triple, while RSFT selects precisely the gold evidence sentences. For \emph{America's Sweetheart}, SFT cites 6 sentences (precision 0.17) whereas RSFT cites only the single correct sentence (precision 1.00). The pattern is consistent: SFT achieves evidence precision of 0.00--0.20 across these examples, while RSFT achieves 1.00 in all three cases.
424	
425	\begin{table}[t]
426	\centering
427	\caption{Evidence citation comparison between SFT and RSFT. Precision measures the fraction of cited sentences that match gold evidence.}
428	\label{tab:case_evidence}
429	\small
430	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
431	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
432	\toprule
433	\textbf{Document} & \textbf{Triple} & \textbf{SFT Prec.} & \textbf{RSFT Prec.} & \textbf{SFT \#Cited} \\
```

```json
          " Under QLoRA 4-bit quantization ($G{=}4$), GRPO drives Rel-F1 to 1.38 while Evi-F1 rises to 85.04 (EDCR 0.9876): quantization-constrained precision limits the effective parameter space, so the reward signal preferentially optimizes the easier sub-task~\\citep{gao2023scaling}, resembling the quantized-actor divergence of \\citet{li2026qurl}. Full-precision bf16 ($G{=}8$) avoids catastrophe (Rel-F1 45.49, EDCR 0.6318), confirming quantization precision rather than group size as the primary factor."
```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "09ab89ff-b08e-4296-82e5-562603794a1b",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_6/projects/faith-evidence-ie/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_6/projects/faith-evidence-ie/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\subsection{Document-Level Relation Extraction}\n\\label{sec:rw_docre}\n\nDocument-level relation extraction (DocRE) requires reasoning across sentence boundaries to identify relations between entity pairs.\nDocRED \\citep{yao2019docred} established the first large-scale benchmark for this task, catalyzing a line of encoder-based methods that treat DocRE as classification over entity pair representations.\nATLOP \\citep{zhou2021atlop} introduced adaptive thresholding with localized context pooling, and graph-based methods such as GAIN \\citep{zeng2020gain} model inter-sentence reasoning through heterogeneous mention-level and entity-level graphs.\nA parallel thread incorporates sentence-level evidence into these architectures.\nSAIS \\citep{xiao2022sais} introduced evidence supervision as an auxiliary training signal and showed that joint evidence prediction improves extraction through data augmentation and ensemble inference.\nDREEAM \\citep{ma2023dreeam} extended this approach with dynamic memory-based evidence retrieval that guides attention to supporting sentences.\nEIDER \\citep{xie2022eider} jointly trains an evidence extractor and fuses evidence representations into the relation classifier.\nIn all three systems, evidence identification is binary sentence classification over a fixed candidate set, a formulation that adds no output complexity.\nRecent work has recast DocRE for large language models.\nAutoRE \\citep{autore2024} fine-tunes LLMs with QLoRA using a relation-head-facts decomposition; \\citet{xu2024lmrc} frame extraction as multi-choice reading comprehension; RelPrior \\citep{wang2025relprior} uses relation priors to guide LLM-based extraction; GLiDRE \\citep{zhang2025glidre} presents a generalist lightweight approach; Ru-LLM \\citep{wang2025rullm} incorporates logical rule constraints; and \\citet{chen2025docksrag} combine retrieval-augmented generation with structured knowledge.\nThese methods match or surpass encoder baselines but produce bare triplets without evidence. Among LLM-based generative methods at the 8B scale, our RSFT model (54.33 Ign-F1 on DocRED dev) outperforms AutoRE (52.14\\footnote{AutoRE Ign-F1 is from the LLaMA3-8B reproduction by \\citet{wang2025relprior}; the original paper reports only F1.}) and is slightly below RelPrior ($-$1.17pp), while being the only method that additionally provides sentence-level evidence grounding.\nThe critical distinction lies in computational nature: in SAIS and DREEAM, evidence retrieval is classification that regularizes the encoder without increasing output length; in LLM-based DocRE, evidence requires additional output tokens per triple, imposing costs that depend on model capacity and optimization strategy.\nWhether this cost is inevitable or can be mitigated is the central question of our work.\n\n\\subsection{Reinforcement Learning for Structured Generation}\n\\label{sec:rw_rl}\n\nGroup Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) \\citep{shao2024deepseekmath} replaces the value network of PPO \\citep{schulman2017ppo} by computing advantages within groups.\nSubsequent work addresses failure modes: DAPO \\citep{yu2025dapo} prevents entropy collapse, Dr.\\ GRPO \\citep{liu2025drgrpo} corrects length bias, $\\mu$-GRPO \\citep{tian2026mugrpo} improves off-policy efficiency, Tree-GRPO \\citep{ji2026treegrpo} extends GRPO to agentic settings, and multi-objective variants \\citep{liu2026gdpo,ito2025mogrpo} handle competing reward signals.\nApplications to structured IE include R1-RE \\citep{dai2025r1re} and REAR \\citep{wang2025rear}.\nDirect Preference Optimization \\citep{rafailov2023direct} reformulates Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback \\citep[RLHF;][]{ouyang2022instructgpt} as implicit reward maximization; KTO \\citep{ethayarajh2024kto} further simplifies alignment by learning from binary desirability signals without paired preferences.\nOur decoupling spectrum is distinct from catastrophic forgetting in RL alignment \\citep{jin2025rl_panacea}, where models lose pre-trained capabilities across tasks; in our setting, extraction and evidence compete \\emph{within} a single task, resembling negative transfer in multi-task learning \\citep{ruder2017overview,standley2020tasks,yu2020gradient}, amplified by QLoRA's \\citep{dettmers2023qlora} constrained parameter space and capacity-dependent scaling dynamics \\citep{hoffmann2022chinchilla,gao2023scaling,moskovitz2024constrained,coste2024reward}.\n\n\\subsection{Evidence Faithfulness and Rejection Sampling}\n\\label{sec:rw_faithfulness}\n\nEvidence faithfulness evaluation has received increasing attention: \\citet{laban2022summac} re-visit NLI-based models for summarization inconsistency detection, \\citet{li2025drift} propose dual-reward inference for faithful rationale generation, \\citet{li2025srvf} use supervised rationale verification with contrastive feedback for relation extraction, \\citet{zhang2024infuse} introduce fine-grained NLI-based faithfulness metrics for summarization, and EvidenceRL \\citep{tamo2026evidencerl} reinforces evidence consistency through GRPO-based optimization with grounding rewards.\nOur CED scoring builds on similar NLI-based principles but targets evidence-relation entailment rather than free-form rationales.\n\n\\citet{zelikman2022star} introduced STaR for iterative self-improvement; \\citet{yuan2023rft} demonstrate the scaling properties of rejection sampling fine-tuning; \\citet{dong2023raft} generalize with reward-model-based sample filtering; and recent analysis shows rejection sampling implicitly performs PPO-like optimization \\citep{tang2025rsftppo}. Extensions include RIFT \\citep{liu2026rift}, which repurposes rejected samples, and TrajFusion \\citep{deng2026trajfusion}, which fuses trajectories beyond simple selection.\nRSFT avoids intra-task competition by operating on discrete outputs rather than gradients, extending the sample-and-select principle \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency} to structured IE with joint quality scoring.\nBecause RSFT selects complete outputs rather than differentiating through a reward signal, it cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off that gradient methods discover, inherently preserving joint quality.\n",
        "numLines": 39,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 39
      }
    },

  },
```

```json
{
```
